They tell me which photographs belong in the archive. It’s like women whispering from behind the walls, and I find myself paying attention to that, especially in my apartment or when I’m in empty museum rooms.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something that might be harmful to yourself or others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed them telling me to hurt myself or anyone else. I lose the thread very easily. If I’m trying to read a letter or talk with my cousin, something small like a siren outside, the refrigerator humming, or even a bright color in a brochure will pull me away. Then I forget what I was doing or what I meant to say, almost right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with your daily routines or personal care, like keeping yourself clean or managing your appearance?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. I haven’t really been keeping track of it. Some days I manage okay, but I don’t know if I’m doing things as regularly as I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your living space organized?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my place gets messy sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s more than normal. I haven’t been paying close attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty maintaining a regular daily schedule?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I miss appointments and meetings, and I’ve missed bills too. I used to be able to keep track of things, but now my cousin has had to help me, and even someone from the museum had to step in around public events because I couldn’t manage it safely on my own. It’s embarrassing, but I can’t really keep a regular schedule by myself right now.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as hearing voices or having disorganized thoughts, caused you significant problems in your relationships, work, or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s caused real problems. I’ve lost trust with colleagues because I haven’t been reliable. I’ve missed board meetings, and I’ve missed bills. My cousin has had to help me with appointments and money, and someone at the museum had to step in around public events because I wasn’t managing them safely on my own. It’s been pretty serious.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling depressed or excessively happy, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes. I have these days where I feel almost radiant, like I’ve been chosen for something important. And then if someone questions what I’m doing, or tells me to slow down, I get furious. It feels like they’re trying to erase me or stop me from being who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or the tasks they tell you to do are based on reality or something that is actually true?\n\nPatient: Yes. I believe it’s real. I’m convinced the museum board and the city arts people have been monitoring my phone because they know my Caribbean archive will change how the galleries tell the truth. No one has shown me anything that proves otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices or the tasks they give you are something that other people can also hear or experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t think everyone hears them, no. But to me they’re very clear. I hear women whispering in my apartment, and sometimes in empty rooms at the museum, like they’re behind the walls. They tell me which photographs belong in the archive.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special mission or are exceptionally talented in a way that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do feel that way sometimes. I get these days where I feel radiant, like I’ve been chosen for something important. And if someone questions my plan or tells me to slow down, I get very angry, like they’re trying to erase me or stop what I’m meant to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your belief about the museum board and city arts people monitoring your phone might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that’s possible. I know how it sounds, but I’m convinced they’ve been monitoring my phone. The museum board, the city arts people — they know what my Caribbean archive means, and they don’t want that truth in the galleries. No one has shown me anything that proves I’m wrong.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices and the tasks they give you related to a specific idea or goal that you feel is very important?\n\nPatient: Yes. They’re tied to the archive. The voices tell me which photographs belong there, like the women are whispering from behind the walls. It feels important—like I’m supposed to get those photographs in the right place.\n\nDoctor: Are all the voices and ideas you've been having centered around the museum and the archive, or are there other unrelated topics they discuss?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s about the museum and the archive. I keep hearing the voices in my apartment, and sometimes in empty museum rooms, like women whispering from behind the walls. They tell me which photographs belong in the archive. I don’t really hear them talking about other things much.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your archive will have a significantly greater impact on the world than what others might expect?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do believe that. I think the archive will change how the galleries tell the truth, especially about Caribbean women and what’s been left out. That’s why I’m convinced the museum board and city arts people are watching my phone — they know it matters more than people expect. No one has shown me anything that proves otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Can anything or anyone change your mind about the museum board and city arts people monitoring your phone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, someone would have to actually show me proof that they’re not watching it, and no one has. I’m convinced the museum board and the city arts people are monitoring my phone because they know the Caribbean archive will change how the galleries tell the truth. So no, not really.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

I miss appointments and meetings, and I’ve missed bills too. I used to be able to keep track of things, but now my cousin has had to help me, and even someone from the museum had to step in around public events because I couldn’t manage it safely on my own. It’s embarrassing, but I can’t really keep a regular schedule by myself right now.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as hearing voices or having disorganized thoughts, caused you significant problems in your relationships, work, or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s caused real problems. I’ve lost trust with colleagues because I haven’t been reliable. I’ve missed board meetings, and I’ve missed bills. My cousin has had to help me with appointments and money, and someone at the museum had to step in around public events because I wasn’t managing them safely on my own. It’s been pretty serious.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling depressed or excessively happy, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes. I have these days where I feel almost radiant, like I’ve been chosen for something important. And then if someone questions what I’m doing, or tells me to slow down, I get furious. It feels like they’re trying to erase me or stop me from being who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or the tasks they tell you to do are based on reality or something that is actually true?\n\nPatient: Yes. I believe it’s real. I’m convinced the museum board and the city arts people have been monitoring my phone because they know my Caribbean archive will change how the galleries tell the truth. No one has shown me anything that proves otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices or the tasks they give you are something that other people can also hear or experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t think everyone hears them, no. But to me they’re very clear. I hear women whispering in my apartment, and sometimes in empty rooms at the museum, like they’re behind the walls. They tell me which photographs belong in the archive.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special mission or are exceptionally talented in a way that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do feel that way sometimes. I get these days where I feel radiant, like I’ve been chosen for something important. And if someone questions my plan or tells me to slow down, I get very angry, like they’re trying to erase me or stop what I’m meant to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your belief about the museum board and city arts people monitoring your phone might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that’s possible. I know how it sounds, but I’m convinced they’ve been monitoring my phone. The museum board, the city arts people — they know what my Caribbean archive means, and they don’t want that truth in the galleries. No one has shown me anything that proves I’m wrong.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices and the tasks they give you related to a specific idea or goal that you feel is very important?\n\nPatient: Yes. They’re tied to the archive. The voices tell me which photographs belong there, like the women are whispering from behind the walls. It feels important—like I’m supposed to get those photographs in the right place.\n\nDoctor: Are all the voices and ideas you've been having centered around the museum and the archive, or are there other unrelated topics they discuss?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s about the museum and the archive. I keep hearing the voices in my apartment, and sometimes in empty museum rooms, like women whispering from behind the walls. They tell me which photographs belong in the archive. I don’t really hear them talking about other things much.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your archive will have a significantly greater impact on the world than what others might expect?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do believe that. I think the archive will change how the galleries tell the truth, especially about Caribbean women and what’s been left out. That’s why I’m convinced the museum board and city arts people are watching my phone — they know it matters more than people expect. No one has shown me anything that proves otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Can anything or anyone change your mind about the museum board and city arts people monitoring your phone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, someone would have to actually show me proof that they’re not watching it, and no one has. I’m convinced the museum board and the city arts people are monitoring my phone because they know the Caribbean archive will change how the galleries tell the truth. It’s embarrassing, but I can’t really keep a regular schedule by myself right now.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as hearing voices or having disorganized thoughts, caused you significant problems in your relationships, work, or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s caused real problems. I’ve lost trust with colleagues because I haven’t been reliable. I’ve missed board meetings, and I’ve missed bills. My cousin has had to help me with appointments and money, and someone at the museum had to step in around public events because I wasn’t managing them safely on my own. It’s been pretty serious.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your mood, such as feeling depressed or excessively happy, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes. I have these days where I feel almost radiant, like I’ve been chosen for something important. And then if someone questions what I’m doing, or tells me to slow down, I get furious. It feels like they’re trying to erase me or stop me from being who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or the tasks they tell you to do are based on reality or something that is actually true?\n\nPatient: Yes. I believe it’s real. I’m convinced the museum board and the city arts people have been monitoring my phone because they know my Caribbean archive will change how the galleries tell the truth. No one has shown me anything that proves otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices or the tasks they give you are something that other people can also hear or experience?\n\nPatient: I don’t think everyone hears them, no. But to me they’re very clear. I hear women whispering in my apartment, and sometimes in empty rooms at the museum, like they’re behind the walls. They tell me which photographs belong in the archive.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special mission or are exceptionally talented in a way that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do feel that way sometimes. I get these days where I feel radiant, like I’ve been chosen for something important. And if someone questions my plan or tells me to slow down, I get very angry, like they’re trying to erase me or stop what I’m meant to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your belief about the museum board and city arts people monitoring your phone might not be true?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that’s possible. I know how it sounds, but I’m convinced they’ve been monitoring my phone. The museum board, the city arts people — they know what my Caribbean archive means, and they don’t want that truth in the galleries. No one has shown me anything that proves I’m wrong.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices and the tasks they give you related to a specific idea or goal that you feel is very important?\n\nPatient: Yes. They’re tied to the archive. The voices tell me which photographs belong there, like the women are whispering from behind the walls. It feels important—like I’m supposed to get those photographs in the right place.\n\nDoctor: Are all the voices and ideas you've been having centered around the museum and the archive, or are there other unrelated topics they discuss?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s about the museum and the archive. I keep hearing the voices in my apartment, and sometimes in empty museum rooms, like women whispering from behind the walls. They tell me which photographs belong in the archive. I don’t really hear them talking about other things much.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your archive will have a significantly greater impact on the world than what others might expect?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do believe that. I think the archive will change how the galleries tell the truth, especially about Caribbean women and what’s been left out. That’s why I’m convinced the museum board and city arts people are watching my phone — they know it matters more than people expect. No one has shown me anything that proves otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Can anything or anyone change your mind about the museum board and city arts people monitoring your phone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, someone would have to actually show me proof that they’re not watching it, and no one has. I’m convinced the museum board and the city arts people are monitoring my phone because they know the Caribbean archive will change how the galleries tell the truth. So no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could potentially change your belief about the museum board and city arts people monitoring your phone?\n\nPatient: I really don’t think so. I’m convinced they’re monitoring my phone because they know what my Caribbean archive would do—how it would change the way the galleries tell the truth. No one has shown me anything that proves they aren’t watching it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that the museum board and city arts people are monitoring your phone is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it could happen in real life. People monitor phones all the time, especially when something important is involved. And I’m convinced the museum board and city arts people are doing it because they know my Caribbean archive could change how the galleries tell the truth. No one has shown me anything that proves they aren’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
